<message id="468" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jun 2, 2026, 12:26 PM">Sortiere meine Gedanken. Ich muss eine Recherche mit perplexity zur Zukunft der produktmanager im product operating Model Kontext machen. Ein white paper schreiben. Der pm wird mehr zum erklärer für seine engineers, er wird mehr Strategie erklären, er übernimmt mehr Funktionen seines managers oder das Team wird kleiner und er wird mehr hands on Prototypen bauen um besser zu erklären und validieren und mehr mit dem Kunden reden müssen. So oder so brauche ich eine Ableitung was ich meinen pms dann beibringen muss, was sie selber lernen müssen. Domänenexpertise, judgment, stakeholder management, influencing. Dieses white paper muss ich dann mit meinen Kollegen teilen und validieren. Es soll auch verschiedene Optionen und Szenarien enthalten dieit Beispielen wie andere Firmen arbeiten untermauert sind (aus Lenny podcast transkripten). 
Schreibe einen sauberen prompt damit ich dann in Perplexity eine tiefe Analyse machen kann. Darin enthalten soll auch ein Absatz zu AI native skills sein</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
Hier der Perplexity-Prompt:

---

**Research prompt: The Future of the Product Manager in the Product Operating Model**

I am writing a white paper on how the role of the Product Manager is evolving within the context of the Product Operating Model (POM). Please conduct a deep analysis covering the following:

---

**1. Core thesis to explore**
As AI and leaner org structures reshape product teams, the PM role is bifurcating. Analyze the following scenarios:

- **Scenario A – The Explainer PM:** The PM becomes primarily a strategic translator — explaining vision, goals, and context to engineers who are increasingly autonomous. Less feature spec, more narrative and alignment.
- **Scenario B – The Strategic PM:** The PM absorbs more responsibilities traditionally owned by the Product Director or GPM. Smaller teams, wider scope, more P&L and business ownership.
- **Scenario C – The Builder PM:** The PM builds hands-on prototypes and demos to validate ideas faster and communicate more concretely with customers and engineers. Vibe coding and low-code tools become core skills.
- **Scenario D – The Customer PM:** The PM spends significantly more time in direct customer contact, replacing some of the work that could previously be delegated to researchers or customer success teams.

For each scenario: What triggers it? What does the PM's day look like? What skills are required? Are these scenarios mutually exclusive or concurrent?

---

**2. What PMs must learn — skill implications per scenario**
For each scenario above, derive the key skills PMs need to develop or deepen:
- Domain expertise (technical, industry, business model)
- Judgment (what to build, what not to build, when to stop)
- Stakeholder management and influence without authority
- Communication and storytelling for diverse audiences
- AI-native skills (see section 4)

---

**3. How leading companies are already working this way**
Provide concrete examples from companies known for str...

2) regelmäßiges Seminar zum Austausch, ein Video pro Woche mit Input und der nächsten Aufgabe, Coachingtermin wöchentlich, Peer group wöchentlich 
3) Firmenstruktur: Andi, tony, Steffen in holding, bekommt von jeder Firma x%, Maryna&amp;Andi Firmengründungsfirma, bekommt von jeder Firma y%, coach bekommt z%, Gründer (student) bekommt von jeder Firma 50%.</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
**Seminar-Struktur: "How to Found in the Age of AI"**

---

**1. Konzept & Versprechen**
Jeder Teilnehmer bekommt:
- 1.000 € Startkapital
- Technische Infrastruktur (Hosting, Tools, Stack)
- Eine echte GmbH / UG mit Domain

**Core Beliefs (framing for the entire paper)**
This white paper is grounded in the following convictions:
- **The Product Operating Model makes more sense than ever.** As AI accelerates execution, the need for clear ownership, empowered teams, and outcome-driven structure intensifies — not diminishes.
- **Outcome over output.** The shift from shipping features to delivering measurable value is the defining discipline of modern product work, and AI makes it easier to ship more while making it harder to know if it matters.
- **Build to learn, not to earn.** Prototyping and product discovery are not about validating assumptions cheaply — they are a fundamental mindset shift: the primary purpose of building is to learn faster.
- **Product sense is a competitive advantage.** In a world where execution is increasingly commoditized by AI, judgment — knowing what to build, for whom, and why — becomes the scarce and differentiating skill.

---

- **Scenario B – The Strategic PM:** The PM absorbs responsibilities traditionally owned by the Product Director or GPM. Smaller teams, wider scope, more P&L and business ownership.

<message id="500" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jun 3, 2026, 11:43 AM">Add this as well What is the role of the PRD? 

The PRD plays different roles in the project model versus the product model.  

In the product model, once we have discovered an effective solution (build to learn), then we need to communicate our learnings to the engineers so they are clear on what they need to deliver (build to earn).  

The primary way we communicate what needs to be built by the engineers is via the prototype we created in product discovery.  This is referred to as “prototype as spec.”  

That said, there are often additional aspects of the specification that are not easily communicated in a prototype, and these are spelled out in the PRD.  This is usually an enumeration of specific use cases, and non-functional requirements (e.g. what are the scale expectations?).

What is most important is that the PRD not be used instead of product discovery, in which case you are back to the project model.  

In the product model, a good PRD supplements product discovery.  In the project model, the PRD is done in lieu of product discovery.

Countless products have failed because a product manager thinks he or she knows what’s “required” only to find later that they were wrong.</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
Ergänzt als neuer Abschnitt im Prompt:

---

**9. The Role of the PRD in the Product vs. Project Model**

The PRD plays fundamentally different roles depending on which model a team operates in. Analyze this distinction and its implications:

**In the Product Model:**
Once product discovery has surfaced an effective solution (*build to learn*), the team moves to delivery (*build to earn*). The primary communication artifact is the **prototype as spec** — the prototype built during discovery communicates what needs to be built. The PRD supplements the prototype by enumerating:
- Specific use cases not easily captured in a prototype
- Non-functional requirements (e.g. scale, performance, security expectations)

The critical constraint: **the PRD must never replace product discovery.** If it does, the team has reverted to the project model.

**In the Project Model:**
The PRD is written *in lieu of* product discovery. The PM assumes they know what is "required" — without validating with customers or through prototyping. This is one of the most common root causes of product failure.

**Key questions to explore:**
- How are leading product organizations using the PRD differently today vs. five years ago?
- How does AI change the PRD — does it make specless development more feasible, or does it increase the risk of skipping discovery?
- What does "prototype as spec" look like in practice at companies like Linear, Figma, or Notion?
- How do you coach PMs to resist the pressure to write a PRD before they've learned anything?

**Core belief to validate:** Countless products have failed because a PM believed they knew what was required — only to discover they were wrong. The PRD is a delivery artifact, not a discovery substitute.

---

**Research prompt: How Product Leadership Evolves in the Age of AI**

This white paper is the companion to a first paper focused on individual PMs. If WP1 is about what PMs must become, WP2 is about what their leaders must become to enable that.

---

**Core Beliefs**
- The Product Operating Model makes more sense than ever — and product leaders are its guardians.
- Leadership in the age of AI is less about decisions and more about context, principles, and connections.
- The moat is increasingly in distribution — product leaders must bridge product and go-to-market.
- Leading by principles scales; leading by presence does not.
- Psychological safety and mental health are not soft skills — they are structural leadership responsibilities.

---

**1. What stays from the Product Operating Model**

- Empowered teams over feature teams
- Cascading strategy: teams receive context, not tasks
- Coaching over managing
- Protecting discovery from delivery pressure
- Portfolio prioritization across teams
- Being the cultural carrier of the Product Operating Model

- **The Connector:** Creates connections between people and ...

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">---

**WHITE PAPER 1: The Future of the Product Manager**
*(Zielgruppe: Team PMs / ICs)*

Research prompt: The Future of the Product Manager in the Product Operating Model

I am writing a white paper on how the role of the Product Manager is evolving within the context of the Product Operating Model (POM). The ideas below are my initial hypotheses — please challenge them where evidence or practice contradicts them.

**Core Beliefs**
- The Product Operating Model makes more sense than ever. As AI accelerates execution, the need for clear ownership, empowered teams, and outcome-driven structure intensifies — not diminishes.
- Outcome over output. The shift from shipping features to delivering measurable value is the defining discipline of modern product work.
- Build to learn, not to earn. Prototyping and product discovery are a fundamental mindset shift: the primary purpose of building is to learn faster.
- Product sense is a competitive advantage. In a world where execution is increasingly commoditized by AI, judgment — knowing what to build, for whom, and why — becomes the scarce and differentiating skill.
- Agency is a learnable discipline. PMs who act without waiting for permission, clarity, or perfect information create disproportionate value.
- Learning by doing beats learning by reading. The 24/7 AI coach enables a new kind of fast, practice-based, self-directed learning.
- Strong leadership ≠ command-and-control. Real leadership — and good PM work — is about setting direction and guardrails, not dictating every decision. Decisions should sit with the person closest to the problem.
- The "flotilla of kayaks" model: teams move in an aligned direction while retaining independent exploration. Consultative decision-making — one person decides, incorporating input from relevant stakeholders — is the operating mode of empowered teams.

**1. Core thesis to explore**
As AI and leaner org structures reshape product teams, the PM role is bifurc...

<message id="688" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jul 9, 2026, 1:51 PM">Kannst du eine Test Nachricht an cgdoering@yahoo.com schicken? Erzähl ihm einen witz</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
E-Mails versenden kann ich leider nicht — ich hab keinen E-Mail-Tool in meiner Toolbox. Ich kann nur Nachrichten an die konfigurierten Kanäle schicken (Signal, Discord, andere Agents).

Falls du das brauchst, könnte man mir ein E-Mail-MCP-Tool einrichten (z.B. via SMTP oder Gmail API).
</message>

**User**: <context timezone="Europe/Berlin" />
<message id="690" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jul 9, 2026, 2:11 PM">Probier es jetzt nochmal, du hast einen MCP dafur</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
E-Mail-Tool ist da, aber die Anmeldung schlägt fehl — der SMTP-Login für familielindenblatt@posteo.de ist nicht autorisiert. Die Zugangsdaten im E-Mail-MCP müssen noch konfiguriert werden (App-Passwort oder SMTP-Credentials für Posteo).
</message>

**User**: <context timezone="Europe/Berlin" />
<message id="692" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jul 9, 2026, 2:14 PM">Probiere nochmal</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
Gleicher Fehler — Authentication failed. Der SMTP-Login für Posteo klappt nicht. Du müsstest im Posteo-Konto ein App-Passwort erstellen und das im E-Mail-MCP hinterlegen. Soll ich dir erklären wie?
</message>

**User**: <context timezone="Europe/Berlin" />
<message id="694" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jul 9, 2026, 2:16 PM">Das scheint korrekt zu sein. Teste nochmal famlindenblatt@posteo.de</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
Noch derselbe Fehler — der SMTP-Login schlägt fehl, unabhängig vom Empfänger. Das Problem liegt beim Absenderkonto (familielindenblatt@posteo.de): Der Server lehnt die Anmeldung ab. 

Posteo erfordert für externe Apps ein separates App-Passwort — das normale Posteo-Passwort funktioniert nicht per SMTP. Das müsste unter posteo.de → Einstellungen → Sicherheit → E-Mail-Passwort generiert und im MCP hinterlegt werden.
</message>

<message id="698" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jul 9, 2026, 3:20 PM">Schreib mir eine Nachricht vor die mein Freund Henje seiner Ehefrau Lene schicken kann um ihr zu zeigen, dass er ans sie denkt. Wir sind gerade am Gardasee angekommen</message>

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
Hier ein paar Optionen, je nach Stil:

**Kurz & herzlich:**
"Hey Lene, wir sind gerade am Gardasee angekommen — und ich denke sofort an dich. Wünschte, du wärst hier. ❤️"

**Etwas romantischer:**
"Lene, der Gardasee ist wunderschön — aber ohne dich fehlt das Beste. Ich denke an dich. Bis bald! 😘"

**Mit einem kleinen Augenzwinkern:**
"Am Gardasee angekommen. Sonne, Berge, Wasser… und ich denke trotzdem nur an dich. Du hast mich ruiniert. 😄❤️"

Henje kann einfach die nehmen, die am besten zu ihm passt!
</message>

<message id="724" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Jul 12, 2026, 10:31 PM">Ich bin Wingsurfen diese Woche. Das wirkt wie ein Training für meinen ganzen Körper. Was kann ich machen Zuhause das ähnlich effektiv ist und Spaß macht. Ich habe keinen see in der Nähe</message>

**Nano**: Wingsurfen belastet vor allem Core, Schultern/Arme (Wing halten), Beine (Stance) und Gleichgewicht. Ein paar Alternativen die ähnlich viel Spaß machen:

**Sehr ähnlich:**
- **Bouldern** — voller Körpereinsatz, Problemlösung, sozialer Spaß, keine Ausrüstung zu Hause nötig. Nächste Halle reicht. Vermutlich das Beste 1:1-Ersatz.
- **Balance Board / Indo Board** — simuliert direkt das Boardgefühl, Core nonstop aktiv, kann man zu Hause aufstellen.

**Mit dem was du schon hast:**
- **Slackline** (habt ihr laut Packliste) — aufbauen im Garten, Gleichgewicht und Core, erstaunlich anstrengend
- **Trampolin** (habt ihr laut Packliste) — Koordination, Körperspannung, macht auch Kindern Spaß

**Wenn du es strukturierter magst:**
- **Ringe/TRX** — Schultern und Core wie beim Wing halten, viel intensiver als es aussieht

Meine Empfehlung: Bouldern als Hauptsache (2x/Woche), Balance Board zu Hause für die kurzen Sessions dazwischen. Kombiniert treffen die fast alles was Wingsurfen trainiert — und machen beide für sich schon Spaß.

<message to="andi-signal">
Wingsurfen = Core, Schultern, Beine, Balance. Beste Alternativen ohne See:

**Am ähnlichsten:**
- Bouldern — voller Körpereinsatz, macht süchtig, nächste Halle reicht
- Balance Board zu Hause — simuliert direkt das Boardgefühl

**Mit dem was ihr schon habt:**
- Slackline (habt ihr!) — Gleichgewicht + Core, erstaunlich anstrengend
- Trampolin (habt ihr!) — Koordination, Körperspannung

Mein Tipp: Bouldern 2x/Woche + Balance Board für zwischendurch. Trifft fast alles was Wingsurfen trainiert — und macht eigenständig Spaß.
</message>

**User**: This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Ongoing packing list management**: Andi described a 3-phase trip (MTB bus weekend with Felix, hotel week in Sölden, hiking weekend with Suse + girls) and asked for a packing list based on templates. Throughout many messages, Andi updated the list by checking off blocks, adding missing items, and requesting individual messages to react with emojis.
   - **Template maintenance**: Whenever new items were added to the current trip list, they should also be added to `busreise-template.md`.
   - **CalDAV scheduled checks**: Twice-daily automated task to check Posteo calendar for completed tasks and archive them in `todos.md`.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **CalDAV** via `mcp__caldav__list_tasks` (calendar: `andi`, includeCompleted: true)
   - **Signal messaging** via `mcp__nanoclaw__send_message` to `andi-signal`
   - **MCP server reconnection pattern**: Use `ToolSearch` with `select:tool_name` to reload schemas after disconnects
   - **PARA workspace**: `/workspace/extra/familypara/` for family group files
   - **No status messages rule**: Never send pure confirmation/status messages to andi-signal
   - **Dual-file update rule**: When adding new items, always update both the current trip list AND the template
   - **Signal reactions limitation**: Reactions arrive as "[Reaktion 👍 von Andreas auf deine Nachricht]" WITHOUT specifying which message — cannot track which item was reacted to

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/workspace/extra/familypara/03_Areas/Urlaube/packlisten/2026_07_Sommer.md`**
     - Main packing list for Sommerurlaub 2026, 219 lines
     - Current state: nearly everything [x], only open items are:
       - Badezimmer: Toilettenpapier, Nagelknipser, Kindershampoo, Shampoo, Haarbürste, Zahnbürsten etc., Rasierer
       - Elektronik: Laptops, Laptop Lad...

<message id="1026" from="andi-signal" sender="+4917663204022" time="Aug 13, 2026, 12:24 PM">Die Nachricht oben Hollow Body Hold – 45 Sekunden
Fokus: Unterer Rücken bleibt fest in den Boden gepresst. Beine und Oberkörper leicht anheben.
V-Ups (Klappmesser) – 12 Wiederholungen (Alternative zu Leg Raises)
Fokus: Aus dem flachen Liegen gleichzeitig Oberkörper und Beine kontrolliert nach oben führen und die Zehen berühren.
Walkout Planks (Inchworms) – 10 Wiederholungen (Alternative zu Ab Rollout)
Fokus: Aus dem Stand mit den Händen nach vorne wandern, bis du weit über die normale Plank-Position hinausreichst (Spannung halten!), dann wieder zurückwandern.</message>

**User**: [Your previous response had no visible output. Please continue and produce a user-visible response.]

**Nano**: <message to="andi-signal">
*Fitnessplan: Core Circuit*

3 Übungen, 3–4 Runden, 2–3× pro Woche (z.B. Mo / Mi / Fr)

──────────────────
*Runde (3–4×):*

1️⃣ Hollow Body Hold
   45 Sek. halten
   → Pause: 15 Sek.

2️⃣ V-Ups (Klappmesser)
   12 Wdh. kontrolliert
   → Pause: 30 Sek.

3️⃣ Walkout Planks (Inchworms)
   10 Wdh. langsam
   → Pause: 60 Sek., dann nächste Runde

──────────────────
*Progression (alle 2 Wochen):*

Woche 1–2: 3 Runden
Woche 3–4: 4 Runden
Woche 5–6: Hollow Body auf 60 Sek., V-Ups auf 15 Wdh., Inchworms auf 12 Wdh.

*Gesamt pro Einheit:* ca. 15–20 Min.
</message>
